- <a id="revision-19ef372cdea487cd584475c3fb4038fc0e59a94420f7c2ff7d324215a426968b"></a>**cross-asset-commodity-trend-2026-11-10** (2026-11-10): UNRESOLVED / CLOSE THIS SPECIFICATION. Protocol update: unchanged. The report states that the ETF-only 14-single-commodity universe, 12–1 momentum with volatility filter, and top-N/threshold WFO produced OOS Sharpe 0.13 vs SPY 0.74, CAGR 1.0%, MaxDD −37.0% vs −33.7%, 27/50 gates, and an active regime halt. Preserve the original NO-GO. The audit found no raw artifact or brief, anchor-leak contamination that may favor the candidate, duplicate-SPY contamination of unclear direction, unknown DSR units, unverifiable code, and no power estimate. These prevent valid protocol and evidence re-determination; they do not make the reported NO-GO proof of no edge. The original report documents closure of this specification; unresolved evidence does not reverse that action. Approval: unknown; Uncertainties: The report has prose performance claims but the audited raw artifact was not found. Audit contamination concerns WFO and its impact is unknown. The tested question is isolated commodity trend/filter versus SPY, not commodity diversification against a fixed passive allocation. Carry and basis-reversal legs were outside scope and remain untested.. Protocol update: unchanged. The report's primary claim is dynamic hedge versus static policy on two ETF pairs, not hedging versus SPY. It reports dynamic Sharpe 0.41, CAGR 6.0%, MaxDD −36.7%, below static 50/50 (0.66, 12.4%, −34.8%) and 100%-hedged (0.73, 14.7%, −35.9%). Preserve the original NO-GO for this retail-proxy rule. The audit found no raw artifact or brief, anchor-leak contamination that may favor the candidate, duplicate-SPY contamination of unclear direction, unknown DSR units, unverifiable code, and no MDE. These faults prevent independent protocol or inferential determination; the result does not establish a general case against dynamic currency hedging. The original report documents closure of this specification; unresolved evidence does not reverse that action. The unhedged reference was 0.53 Sharpe, 9.7% CAGR and -35.2% MaxDD; SPY reference was 0.74, 15.2% and -33.7%. Approval: unknown; Uncertainties: The academic claim and report comparator are dynamic versus static FX hedge policies; SPY is reference-only. The available JPY/EUR ETF proxy history does not test the broader strategy. The audit found no raw artifact, so values were not independently recomputed. No owner-approved continuation is evidenced.. Protocol update: unchanged. The separately frozen 2007-06-01–2011-02-28 holdout met its restricted checks: static 80/20 with DBC had Sharpe 0.172 vs SPY 0.078 and MaxDD −46.3% vs −55.2%; results held at 3 bp. Its brief explicitly calls this weak support because the roughly 3.75-year span is dominated by 2008, likely to flatter the mix. It does not establish material downside improvement plus wealth noninferiority. Preserve PASSED (weak); protocol MET is not proof of persistent defensive effect. The report records CAGR 1.3% versus -1.7% and 2008 total return -29.9% versus -36.8% for SPY. Approval: unknown; Uncertainties: Lens 1 calls the holdout clean and Lens 7 spot-checked recomputation. Lens 6 is unverifiable; Lens 9 cannot compute t-statistic, uncertainty, or MDE. The report records that SPY differed from the OHLCV tape on one overlap day outside the holdout. The period is limited and does not cover varied adverse regimes. The holdout brief states that data from 2011-03 onward had already been seen.. Protocol update: unchanged. The registered trend-preference rule was not met: pinned trend improvement over static was only +0.007 Sharpe and +1.6 MaxDD points versus required +0.05 Sharpe or +3 points without Sharpe loss; WFO halted in folds 2–8, violating the no-halt condition. Long-window trend Sharpe was below static (0.948 vs 0.967). This supports the original narrow conclusion that this timing arm adds nothing over static under its rule, not a general absence of defensive value. The original closure is preserved. The report gives 14/17 WFO gate passes and lookback 126 winning 8/17. The clean pinned comparison alone fails the necessary first condition, independently of uncertain WFO validity. Approval: unknown; Uncertainties: Lens 1 says pinned comparison is clean but WFO validity is unknown (it predates an engine fix); Lens 2 records DSR-unit uncertainty, code unverifiable, and no MDE; Lens 9 cannot compute uncertainty. NOT MET is the registered policy/operational outcome; broad inference is inconclusive.. The corrected replay artifact contains aggregate statistics, not daily return observations for a new independent recomputation. Preserve each decision at its own window and scope; neither record establishes that the dual filter adds value on another book size or period. Approval: unknown; Uncertainties: The +12.2% minimum and “vacuous” statement belong only to the June 12 64-month top-50 record. The October corrected replay is separately represented in the preserved aggregate JSON, but its daily OOS return series is not available here for independent recomputation. Lens 9’s MDE is for annualized mean excess, not a CAGR confidence interval; power is too weak to resolve the +1.0 pp/year hurdle. The French portfolio momentum screen is another distinct specification.. The report proposes monthly rebalancing, while the later tax plan refers to drift-band rebalancing. A qualifying contemporaneous observation record, start and any reset have not been established. The October 18-month B1 standard applies to future stock candidates; no source establishes its retroactive application to this allocation sleeve. The historical static GO for shadow and weak holdout pass remain intact. A governing-rule decision by the owner and qualifying evidence are needed before this stage can be assessed; no action is authorized here. Approval: unknown; Uncertainties: Rule authority, drawdown definition, which book parity applies to, after-tax implementation and the relationship to the later A23 allocation decision are unresolved. ### verdict-stocks-a23-static-leverage

Inventory: lens2-stocks-a23-static-leverage  
Initial protocol determination: MET  
Parent: none  
Source: [original](../research/allocation/static-leverage/2026-09-30-static-fractional-leverage.md)

- <a id="revision-f712c7cf58fdf40d6f43c00b018a5719ca467c4519f2ef805f2849e201937ea8"></a>**review-stocks-a23-static-leverage-2026-11-10** (2026-11-10): INCONCLUSIVE / HOLD / PARK THIS VERSION. Protocol update: unchanged. The registered primary criteria were after-tax CAGR ≥ SPY +1.0 pp/year on the 2006-06-21–2026-09-29 real-SSO window and not below SPY on the 1993-02-01–2006-06-20 synthetic holdout. The report records the 70/30 arm clearing both using the historical financing path; its 2026-09-30 +2.5 pp funding-cost sensitivity reduces the main-window margin to +0.80 pp/year, below the same +1.0 bar. Preserve GO for owner decision and the documented owner adoption of 1.3x; the sensitivity does not authorize changing the allocation. Lens 2 found clean data and recomputation, but code was not examined and Lens 9 could not compute power because the stored artifact lacks the needed uncertainty inputs. The historical result and current funding condition are distinct determinations; no alpha inference is intended. Approval: unknown; Uncertainties: Lens 9 could not compute MDE/power from the stored result fields; this is absence of a computable effect-size uncertainty estimate, not evidence of zero power or invalidity by itself. Lens 6 has no code review row. Tax rates are current-law hypotheticals; synthetic 2x distributions are zero. The rule checks ruin on the full window only. The 30% arm trips ruin and no alternative meets all gates; this is a design limitation explicitly described by the report, not a rationale to alter the dated action..
